## Q1. For split reductive G/𝔽\_(q) and opposite parabolics P,P^(op) sharing Levi M, doe…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 1

For split reductive G/𝔽\_(q) and opposite parabolics P,P^(op) sharing Levi M, does F_(P,P^(op))F_(P^(op),P) act by a power of q on special functions on bar(G/U)(𝔽\_(q))? “Special” means zero sums along the coroot-sum 𝔾\_(m)-orbits. The transform uses the middle-extension gamma-sheaf on the Wang monoid and the Slipper pairing (§4.3). The corresponding sheaf version predicts the identity up to shift and Tate twist.

**Context.** Origin: The thesis proposes these transforms and their inversion conjecture, extending the earlier Braverman–Kazhdan construction.

**Source.** Aaron Arthur Gordon Slipper. *Non-Abelian Fourier Transforms and Normalized Intertwining Operators over Finite Fields for General Parabolic Subgroups*. University of Chicago, 2024. Advisor(s): Ngô Bảo Châu; Kazuya Kato. [primary source](https://knowledge.uchicago.edu/records/af4ec-yjz54/files/Slipper_uchicago_0330D_17473.pdf) · [record](https://knowledge.uchicago.edu/records/af4ec-yjz54) Location: §4.3, unnumbered Function and Sheaf Conjectures, printed pp. 26–30, especially pp. 29–30. Follow-on evidence: Author’s accessible preprint still marks general involutivity conjectural. The 11 July 2026 journal version establishes inversion for specified low-rank/quadric cases, without claiming the general result. No general proof or counterexample located.

**Further links.** [1](https://math.uchicago.edu/~aaronslipper/uploads/Paraspherical%20Draft%20Preprint.pdf) · [2](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s10468-026-10405-5) · [3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.17476)


<a id="q2"></a>

## Q2. Determine the full center of U(𝔤𝔩(m𝟏+nP)) for arbitrary m,n. The case m=0,n=1 is…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 2.10.22) · **Collection** 1

Determine the full center of U(𝔤𝔩(m𝟏+nP)) for arbitrary m,n. The case m=0,n=1 is computed.

**Context.** Origin: These questions arise from Hu’s constructions and calculations, also developed in her 2024–2025 papers; some problems were suggested by her advisor. Shared notation: Over algebraically closed k of characteristic 2, Ver_(4)^(+) is k[δ]/(δ^(2))-modules with primitive δ and braiding 1⊗1+δ⊗δ. Its unit is 𝟏 and its two-dimensional indecomposable projective is P.

**Source.** Serina Hu. *The higher Verlinde category Ver_(4)^(+) and superLie theory in characteristic 2*. Massachusetts Institute of Technology, 2026. Advisor(s): Pavel Etingof. [primary source](https://dspace.mit.edu/server/api/core/bitstreams/145636c9-0cbe-42f6-a4aa-ba2659e3ced2/content) · [record](https://dspace.mit.edu/entities/publication/b7d069f7-e4c7-4350-9779-adcc82af5006) Location: Question 2.10.22, printed p. 72. Search-qualified status: The April 2026 thesis leaves the general center as a question. Author’s research page and 2025 Lie-algebra paper retain it; searched for later center results and found no general resolution. Checked the September 2026 Etingof–Hu preprint: it treats Ver_(p)^(+) for p≥5, a different setting.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/html/2504.01146) · [2](https://serinahu.com/) · [3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01576) · [4](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.18175)

## Q4. For 4-restricted GL(nP) weight λ, 2-restricted GL(m) weight μ, and dominant ν,ρ,…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 3.3.4) · **Collection** 1

For 4-restricted GL(nP) weight λ, 2-restricted GL(m) weight μ, and dominant ν,ρ, is L(μ+2ρ,λχ^(ν))≅L(μ,λ)⊗Frob^(\*)(L_(GL(n))(ν)⊠L_(GL(m))(ρ))? Here restricted means successive weight-degree differences are below 4 or 2; Frob is §1.2.3’s even/odd Frobenius.

**Context.** Origin: These questions arise from Hu’s constructions and calculations, also developed in her 2024–2025 papers; some problems were suggested by her advisor. Shared notation: Over algebraically closed k of characteristic 2, Ver_(4)^(+) is k[δ]/(δ^(2))-modules with primitive δ and braiding 1⊗1+δ⊗δ. Its unit is 𝟏 and its two-dimensional indecomposable projective is P.

**Source.** Serina Hu. *The higher Verlinde category Ver_(4)^(+) and superLie theory in characteristic 2*. Massachusetts Institute of Technology, 2026. Advisor(s): Pavel Etingof. [primary source](https://dspace.mit.edu/server/api/core/bitstreams/145636c9-0cbe-42f6-a4aa-ba2659e3ced2/content) · [record](https://dspace.mit.edu/entities/publication/b7d069f7-e4c7-4350-9779-adcc82af5006) Location: Conjecture 3.3.4, printed p. 88; definitions in §3.3.1. Search-qualified status: The 2026 thesis still calls the formula conjectural; the available research paper likewise does. Targeted author/topic searches and the current publication list revealed no later proof. Checked the September 2026 Etingof–Hu preprint: it treats Ver_(p)^(+) for p≥5, a different setting.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/html/2406.10201) · [2](https://serinahu.com/) · [3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01576) · [4](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.18175)

## Q104. Let G be connected reductive and let λ₁,…,λ\_k be minuscule coweights. For the co…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 3.4.1) · **Collection** 2

Let G be connected reductive and let λ₁,…,λ\_k be minuscule coweights. For the convolution resolution p:Gr^(λ₁)⋆…⋆Gr^(λ\_k)→closure(Gr^(Σλ\_i)), let W be a transversal slice to the closed G(O)-orbit and p′ its induced symplectic resolution. Does a quantization-produced tilting generator on p′ extend to a G(O)⋊G_m-equivariant coherent sheaf on the entire convolution resolution? The GL_n case is proved; the question concerns general G.

**Context.** Origin: Explicit new conjecture in dissertation research with Vasily Krylov. New comparison conjecture between the thesis’s construction and an existing independent construction; joint research with Vasily Krylov.

**Source.** Ilya Dumanski. *Perverse Coherent Sheaves in Representation Theory*. Massachusetts Institute of Technology, 2026. Advisor(s): Roman Bezrukavnikov. [primary source](https://dspace.mit.edu/server/api/core/bitstreams/19fde0d4-e061-4166-a8a0-c0c2ae56c991/content) · [record](https://dspace.mit.edu/entities/publication/a48a82e7-2f3d-47a5-add9-21b2c657ab4a) Location: Conjecture 3.4.1, pp. 76–77. Status evidence, checked 5 October 2026: Their June 2026 preprint repeats this as Conjecture 4.1 and proves only the GL_n case in Proposition 4.4. General case remains conjectural.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.15981)

## Q105. For type-A affine Grassmannian convolution varieties indexed by an arbitrary seq…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 2

For type-A affine Grassmannian convolution varieties indexed by an arbitrary sequence k=(k₁,…,k_m) of minuscule weights, do the thesis’s perversely-exotic t-structure and the Cautis–Koppensteiner t-structure on the equivariant coherent derived category coincide? The thesis observes agreement for k=(1,…,1); it conjectures agreement for all k.

**Context.** Origin: Explicit new conjecture in dissertation research with Vasily Krylov. New comparison conjecture between the thesis’s construction and an existing independent construction; joint research with Vasily Krylov.

## Q108. For an arbitrary symmetric tensor category C of moderate growth, describe the se…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 2.15) · **Collection** 2

For an arbitrary symmetric tensor category C of moderate growth, describe the semiring W(C) of isomorphism classes of nondegenerate symmetric bilinear forms, with orthogonal sum and tensor product. In characteristic 2, also determine the semimodule Q(C) of nondegenerate quadratic forms over W(C). This uses the thesis’s semiring convention, not the usual quotient by metabolic forms.

**Context.** Origin: Question in Kannan’s own joint research with Iz Chen and Krishna Pothapragada. The thesis and paper solve the Ver₄⁺ case while posing the categorical generalization.

**Source.** Arun Shrinath Kannan. *On Lie Theory in the Verlinde Category*. Massachusetts Institute of Technology, 2024. Advisor(s): Pavel Etingof. [primary source](https://dspace.mit.edu/server/api/core/bitstreams/cc50a9ab-2778-4e30-8ad6-2f262a938161/content) · [record](https://calendar.mit.edu/event/thesis-defense-arun-shrinath-kannan) Location: Chapter 4, included article §2, Question 2.15, printed p. 106. Status evidence, checked 5 October 2026: The January 2026 journal article provides the Ver₄⁺ classification, not the arbitrary-category classification. Targeted searches found no resolution of the broader question.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.06712) · [2](https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/abs/pii/S0021869325004843)

## Q118. For every quiver gauge theory in the thesis’s symplectic-resolution setting, is …

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 2.1.6) · **Collection** 2

For every quiver gauge theory in the thesis’s symplectic-resolution setting, is Cν(A_(ℏ,t)(M)) flat over C[t,ℏ]? Here M is the Coulomb branch, t=H²(M̃,C) for its symplectic resolution, A is its universal quantization, and ν is a generic Hamiltonian cocharacter. For the ν-grading A=⊕A_i, the Cartan subquotient is Cν(A)=A₀/Σ\_(i>0)A_(−i)A_i.

**Context.** Origin: A separately formulated auxiliary conjecture in Krylov’s proposed approach, not a repetition of the older Hikita–Nakajima isomorphism conjecture. Its classical flatness version appeared in his joint work with Pavel Shlykov, begun in 2022 and published in 2025. New compatibility conjecture between the dissertation’s own construction and Hikita’s older construction, rather than the already-proved existence of a Hikita isomorphism. Joint Krylov–Shlykov provenance is explicit at the start of §2.2; the question predates filing in their 2022 manuscript.

**Source.** Vasily Krylov. *Geometry and representation theory of symplectic singularities in the context of symplectic duality*. Massachusetts Institute of Technology, 2024. Advisor(s): Roman Bezrukavnikov. [primary source](https://dspace.mit.edu/bitstreams/8c6592d6-48db-4a50-acce-f86724717257/download) · [record](https://dspace.mit.edu/entities/publication/3b7dddcd-45b8-4106-b653-027e505cc3af) Location: Conjecture 2.1.6, printed/PDF p. 34; assumptions pp. 31–32; Cartan-subquotient definition p. 22. Status evidence, checked 5 October 2026: The 2025 Krylov–Shlykov publication retains the classical version as Conjecture 8.3. Dumanski–Krylov (January 2026 revision) and Dinkins–Karpov–Krylov (August 2026) establish further ADE/minuscule-framing and Jordan-quiver cases; neither gives unrestricted flatness for arbitrary quivers. Targeted searches found no general proof or counterexample.

**Further links.** [1](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s00029-025-01028-7) · [2](https://arxiv.org/html/2509.06226) · [3](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.16746) · [4](https://people.math.harvard.edu/~vkrylov/Research.html)

## Q119. For every n≥1, does grΘ:gr Z(CS_n)→C[(Symⁿ A²)^T] equal Hikita’s Section 2 isomo…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 2.2.28) · **Collection** 2

For every n≥1, does grΘ:gr Z(CS_n)→C[(Symⁿ A²)^T] equal Hikita’s Section 2 isomorphism? T acts by t·(x,y)=(tx,t⁻¹y); fixed points are scheme-theoretic. The center’s filtration gives a permutation degree twice its transposition length. Θ identifies a central Specht idempotent with the characteristic function of its partition-labelled Calogero–Moser fixed point, and grΘ is obtained by degeneration.

**Context.** Origin: A separately formulated auxiliary conjecture in Krylov’s proposed approach, not a repetition of the older Hikita–Nakajima isomorphism conjecture. Its classical flatness version appeared in his joint work with Pavel Shlykov, begun in 2022 and published in 2025. New compatibility conjecture between the dissertation’s own construction and Hikita’s older construction, rather than the already-proved existence of a Hikita isomorphism. Joint Krylov–Shlykov provenance is explicit at the start of §2.2; the question predates filing in their 2022 manuscript.

**Source.** Vasily Krylov. *Geometry and representation theory of symplectic singularities in the context of symplectic duality*. Massachusetts Institute of Technology, 2024. Advisor(s): Roman Bezrukavnikov. [primary source](https://dspace.mit.edu/bitstreams/8c6592d6-48db-4a50-acce-f86724717257/download) · [record](https://dspace.mit.edu/entities/publication/3b7dddcd-45b8-4106-b653-027e505cc3af) Location: Conjecture 2.2.28, printed/PDF p. 46; construction of Θ pp. 42–46, especially Theorem 2.2.20 and Corollary 2.2.26. Status evidence, checked 5 October 2026: The March 2025 published paper retains this exact question as Conjecture 2.24. Searches of later publications and Krylov’s current research list found no resolution; the May 2026 K-theory paper concerns a new equivariant K-theory description, not this equality of the older associated-graded maps.

**Further links.** [1](https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s00029-025-01028-7) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/1501.02430) · [3](https://arxiv.org/html/2605.11579) · [4](https://people.math.harvard.edu/~vkrylov/Research.html)

## Q128. Over a characteristic-zero field, let an algebra A satisfy a(bc)=b(ac) and ([a,b…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 2

Over a characteristic-zero field, let an algebra A satisfy a(bc)=b(ac) and ([a,b],c,d)+([b,c],a,d)+([c,a],b,d)=0, where [a,b]=ab−ba and (a,b,c)=a(bc)−(ab)c. Must its commutator algebra A⁽⁻⁾ embed into Z⁽⁻⁾ for some right Zinbiel algebra Z, whose multiplication satisfies x(yz)=(xy+yx)z? Such an embedding means A⁽⁻⁾ is special.

**Context.** Origin: Already posed in the student’s coauthored paper with A. Dzhumadil’daev and N. Ismailov, arXiv:1809.10550 (2018; revised 2019), Section 7, question 2, printed p. 16 in v3. The dissertation reproduces it. The preceding polynomial-ring example is solved and is not this general question.

**Source.** Farukh Arkinovich Mashurov. *Special Tortkara algebras and assosymmetric algebras*. Kazakh-British Technical University, Kazakhstan, 2023. Advisor(s): Askar Dzhumadil’daev; Ivan Shestakov (foreign scientific advisor). [primary source](https://kbtu.edu.kz/images/Mashurov_Dis.pdf) · [record](https://kbtu.edu.kz/images/Mashurov_Dis_2.pdf) Location: Section 2.6, numbered open question 2, printed p. 38 (PDF page could not be independently checked). Identities (14) appear immediately above. Status evidence, checked 5 October 2026: Targeted speciality searches found no resolution of this general ordinary-algebra question. The author’s 2025 paper concerns Zinbiel/Tortkara superalgebras, including exceptional super quotients, not a solution of this ordinary-algebra embedding problem. Title-page advisor data were read through the official indexed PDF; download is presently blocked by a certificate failure, so PDF-page verification remains unavailable.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/1809.10550) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.11382) · [3](https://conferences.cirm-math.fr/3473.html)

## Q202. Does every rational \$q/p\ge1\$ occur as \$\widehat\alpha(I)\$ for a monomial ideal …

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 5.1.2) · **Collection** 3

Does every rational \$q/p\ge1\$ occur as \$\widehat\alpha(I)\$ for a monomial ideal \$I\subset K[x_1,x_2,x_3]\$? If not, determine the excluded values.

**Context.** Origin: New realization and resource-minimization questions following the author’s monomial-ideal constructions. Shared setup for questions 202, 203: Work over a characteristic-zero field \$K\$. For a nonzero proper monomial ideal \$I\subset R=K[x_1,\ldots,x_n]\$, put \$I^{(m)}=\bigcap_{P\in\operatorname{Ass}(R/I)}(I^mR_P\cap R)\$ and \$\widehat\alpha(I)=\lim_{m\to\infty}\alpha(I^{(m)})/m\$, where \$\alpha\$ is least nonzero degree.

**Source.** Craig Kohne. *Monomial ideals with a prescribed Waldschmidt constant*. McMaster University, 2024. Advisor(s): Adam Van Tuyl; Antoine Deza. [primary source](https://macsphere.mcmaster.ca/bitstream/11375/31389/2/Kohne_Craig_S_2024December_PhDMathematics.pdf) · [record](https://ms.mcmaster.ca/~kohnec/) Location: Question 5.1.2, printed p. 85 / PDF p. 96; preceding construction pp. 83–84.

**Further links.** [1](https://ms.mcmaster.ca/~vantuyl/papers.html) · [2](https://arxiv.org/html/2512.22940v1)

## Q212. Let \$A=\bigoplus_{d\ge0}A_d\$ be a unital associative algebra over an algebraical…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 3.10) · **Collection** 3

Let \$A=\bigoplus_{d\ge0}A_d\$ be a unital associative algebra over an algebraically closed field \$k\$, with every \$A_d\$ finite-dimensional. Given primitive idempotents \$e_1,\ldots,e_m\$ satisfying \$e_ie_j=0\$ for \$i\ne j\$ and \$\sum e_i=1\$, must a \$k\$-algebra automorphism carry the entire set to homogeneous idempotents? Such nonzero homogeneous idempotents lie in \$A_0\$.

**Context.** Origin: Question from joint work with Mads Hustad Sandøy; Dramburg posted a precursor on MathOverflow in September 2024.

**Source.** Darius Dramburg. *Higher representation infinite algebras from skew-group algebras: Higher preprojective gradings, Koszul gradings, and McKay quivers*. Uppsala University, 2025. Advisor(s): Martin Herschend. [primary source](https://uu.diva-portal.org/smash/get/diva2%3A1954180/FULLTEXT01.pdf) · [record](https://www.uu.se/en/department/mathematics/events/archive/2025-08-22-dissertation-higher-representation-infinite-algebras-from-skew-group-algebras) Location: §2.5.3, final unnumbered question, printed/PDF p. 31; associated Paper V, Question 3.10.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/html/2609.03288v1) · [2](https://arxiv.org/html/2411.13283) · [3](https://mathoverflow.net/questions/479788/are-idempotents-in-a-nonnegatively-graded-algebra-conjugate-to-homogeneous-idemp)

## Q327. For integers \$n\ge2r\ge2\$, let \$KG(n,r)\$ have the \$r\$-subsets of \$[n]\$ as vertic…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 4

For integers \$n\ge2r\ge2\$, let \$KG(n,r)\$ have the \$r\$-subsets of \$[n]\$ as vertices, adjacent when disjoint. Is \$\chi_q(KG(n,r))=n-2r+2\$? Here \$\chi_q(G)\$ is the least \$c\$ for which some finite \$d\$ admits Hermitian projections \$P_{v,i}\in M_d(\mathbb C)\$ with \$\sum_{i=1}^cP_{v,i}=I_d\$ and \$P_{v,i}P_{w,i}=0\$ whenever \$vw\in E(G)\$. There is no additional cross-vertex commutation requirement.

**Context.** Discovery path (advisor ascent; student → supervisor):
Prem Nigam Kar → David E. Roberson: https://backend.orbit.dtu.dk/ws/portalfiles/portal/432894668/PhD_Thesis_2_.pdf
David E. Roberson → Chris Godsil: https://uwspace.uwaterloo.ca/bitstreams/bdce965a-03ff-412b-a7df-8311f630ff71/download#page=4 Origin: Projective-rank rationality arose with Laura Mančinska in their 2012 preprint. The thesis separately proposes the Kneser quantum-coloring question.

**Source.** David E. Roberson. *Variations on a Theme: Graph Homomorphisms*. University of Waterloo, 2013. Advisor(s): Chris Godsil. ## Q388. Let \$Q\$ be any orientation of the \$E_8\$ Dynkin diagram, and let \$V,W\$ be finite-…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 4

Let \$Q\$ be any orientation of the \$E_8\$ Dynkin diagram, and let \$V,W\$ be finite-dimensional indecomposable representations over a field \$k\$, both nonzero at every vertex. Must the pointwise tensor product \$V\otimes W\$ contain at most one indecomposable direct summand with full support, counted with multiplicity? Equivalently, is the canonical full-support basis of the associated factor of the split representation ring multiplicative, allowing zero products?

**Context.** Discovery path (advisor ascent; student → supervisor):
Darius Dramburg → Martin Herschend: https://uu.diva-portal.org/smash/get/diva2%3A1954180/FULLTEXT01.pdf
Martin Herschend → Ernst Dieterich: https://www.math.nagoya-u.ac.jp/~martinh/cv.pdf Origin: An explicit unknown case singled out from the author’s own representation-ring computation. The thesis proves the corresponding property for E₆ and explains why the same argument works for E₇ but breaks at E₈. It is not the classical Clebsch–Gordan problem for the Lie group E₈.

**Source.** Martin Herschend. *On the Clebsch-Gordan problem for quiver representations*. Uppsala University, 2008. Advisor(s): Ernst Dieterich. [primary source](https://uu.diva-portal.org/smash/get/diva2:171897/FULLTEXT01.pdf) · [record](https://www.uu.se/en/contact-and-organisation/staff?query=N3-1376) Location: §2.6, unnumbered open statement following the multiplicative-basis argument, printed p. 24 / PDF p. 30; Proposition 2.16(2)–(3) gives the equivalent tensor-summand formulation.

**Further links.** [1](https://www.math.nagoya-u.ac.jp/~martinh/cv.pdf) · [2](https://uu.diva-portal.org/smash/get/diva2%3A1954180/FULLTEXT01.pdf) · [3](https://ring-theory-japan.com/ring/oldmeeting/2009/report2009/42Proceedings.pdf)

## Q391. Over an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero, characterize the tupl…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 3.2.10) · **Collection** 4

Over an algebraically closed field of characteristic zero, characterize the tuples of arrays arising as borders of Hilbert functions of finite reduced point sets \$X\subset\mathbb P^{n_1}\times\cdots\times\mathbb P^{n_r}\$. Write \$H_X(j)=\dim(R/I_X)\_j\$, with variables from factor \$i\$ of multidegree \$e_i\$, and \$t_i=|\pi_i(X)|\$. The \$i\$th border array consists of \$H_X(j_1,\ldots,j_{i-1},t_i-1,j_{i+1},\ldots,j_r)\$ for \$0\le j_h&lt;t_h\$. The \$\mathbb P^1\times\mathbb P^1\$ case is already settled in the thesis.

**Context.** Discovery path (advisor ascent; student → supervisor):
Craig Kohne → Adam Van Tuyl: https://macsphere.mcmaster.ca/bitstream/11375/31389/2/Kohne_Craig_S_2024December_PhDMathematics.pdf
Adam Van Tuyl → Anthony V. Geramita: https://ms.mcmaster.ca/~vantuyl/papers/phdthesis.pdf Origin: The thesis introduces the border invariant and immediately poses this realization/classification question. The author’s December 2001 preprint repeats it as Question 4.13. Its P¹×P¹ special case is proved using Gale–Ryser, so that special case is excluded.

**Source.** Adam Van Tuyl. *Sets of Points in Multi-Projective Spaces and their Hilbert Function*. Queen’s University, 2001. Advisor(s): Anthony V. Geramita. [primary source](https://ms.mcmaster.ca/~vantuyl/papers/phdthesis.pdf) · [record](https://ms.mcmaster.ca/~vantuyl/papers.html) Location: Question 3.2.10 and Definition 3.2.8, printed p. 55 / PDF p. 63; overview Question 1.2.5, printed p. 8 / PDF p. 16.

## Q392. Fix a root datum \$R\$. Are there integers \$c\$ and \$p_0\$ depending only on \$R\$ suc…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 5.2.1) · **Collection** 4

Fix a root datum \$R\$. Are there integers \$c\$ and \$p_0\$ depending only on \$R\$ such that, for every connected reductive \$\mathbb F_q\$-group \$\mathbf G\$ with root datum \$R\$ in characteristic \$p>p_0\$, the series \$\zeta(s)=\sum_{\chi\in\operatorname{Irr}(\mathbf G(\mathbb F_q))}\chi(1)^{-s}\$ and \$\epsilon(s)=\sum_{[x]\in\operatorname{Ad}(\mathbf G(\mathbb F_q))\backslash\mathfrak g}|\mathfrak g:C_{\mathfrak g}(x)|^{-s/2}\$, where \$\mathfrak g=\operatorname{Lie}(\mathbf G)(\mathbb F_q)\$, admit presentations \$\sum_i u_i(q)v_i(q)^{-s}\$ with the same number of terms, matching degrees of corresponding \$u_i\$ and \$v_i\$, and all polynomial coefficients in \$(1/c)\mathbb Z\cap[-c,c]\$? Here \$C_{\mathfrak g}(x)\$ is the Lie-algebra centralizer.

**Context.** Metadata note: Submitted October 2018; accepted June 2019 according to the CV; included in the university’s 2020 doctoral graduation booklet. Discovery path (lateral/sibling branch; student → supervisor):
Kie Seng Nge → Uri Onn (associate supervision): https://openresearch-repository.anu.edu.au/server/api/core/bitstreams/4bccb285-e28a-4852-9dda-a190c86c5061/content
Shai Shechter → Uri Onn: https://shaishechter.github.io/Theses/PhD.pdf Origin: Part II reports the student’s ongoing joint project with advisor Uri Onn and formulates this precise comparison relation and conjecture. It is distinct from the older Larsen–Lubotzky conjecture. The proposed intermediate reduction in Question 5.3.1 is not counted separately.

**Source.** Shai Shechter. *On Regularity and Approximative Results Regarding the Representation Zeta Functions of Groups*. Ben-Gurion University of the Negev, 2019. Advisor(s): Uri Onn. [primary source](https://shaishechter.github.io/Theses/PhD.pdf) · [record](https://in.bgu.ac.il/kreitman_school/DocLib1/PHD_2020.pdf) Location: Conjecture 5.2.1, printed p. 61 / PDF p. 79; formulas (5.1)–(5.2), printed p. 59, and Definition 5.1.1, printed p. 60.

## Q393. Does every planar quiver with potential \$(Q,W)\$ over \$\mathbb C\$, with finite-di…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 4

Does every planar quiver with potential \$(Q,W)\$ over \$\mathbb C\$, with finite-dimensional self-injective Jacobian algebra and no interior vertex of valency two, arise from a rotationally symmetric Postnikov strand diagram by deleting boundary vertices, allowing an arbitrary strand-endpoint permutation? Here the Jacobian algebra is the completed path algebra modulo the cyclic derivatives of \$W\$; planar means that attaching a disk along each potential cycle produces a simply connected complex embeddable in the plane.

**Context.** Discovery path (lateral/sibling branch; student → supervisor):
Darius Dramburg → Martin Herschend: https://uu.diva-portal.org/smash/get/diva2%3A1954180/FULLTEXT01.pdf

It is different from the earlier Herschend–Iyama classification question that the thesis disproves.

**Source.** Andrea Pasquali. *Constructions in higher-dimensional Auslander-Reiten theory*. Uppsala University, 2019. Advisor(s): Martin Herschend. [primary source](https://andreapasquali.netlify.app/files/thesis.pdf) · [record](https://www.diva-portal.org/smash/get/diva2%3A1301812/FULLTEXT01.pdf) Location: Introductory essay §4.1, printed/PDF p. 33; construction §3.3, pp. 26–27; Paper V Definition 6.3 gives the planar-canvas definition.

## Q399. For every equicharacteristic local ring \$(A,\mathfrak m,k)\$, is \$\operatorname{e…

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 2.5.24) · **Collection** 4

For every equicharacteristic local ring \$(A,\mathfrak m,k)\$, is \$\operatorname{ecodim}(A)=\operatorname{fcodim}(A)\$? Here \$\operatorname{ecodim}(A)=\operatorname{ht}\ker[\operatorname{Sym}\_k(\mathfrak m/\mathfrak m^2)\to\operatorname{gr}\_{\mathfrak m}(A)]\$, while \$\operatorname{fcodim}(A)\$ is the infimum of \$\operatorname{ht}\ker\tau\$ over continuous surjections \$\tau:k[[x_i\mid i\in I]]\to\widehat A\$ inducing the residue-field identification. \$\widehat A\$ is the \$\mathfrak m\$-adic completion, and the power-series source is the \$(x_i)\$-adic completion of \$k[x_i\mid i\in I]\$, allowing an infinite index set. This is the ordinary \$\mathfrak m\$-adic formulation; the thesis also asks its quasi-preadic analogue.

**Context.** Discovery path (lateral/sibling branch; student → supervisor):
Sergey Yurkevich → Herwig Hauser: https://homepage.univie.ac.at/herwig.hauser/Publications/yurkevich%20dissertation%202023.pdf
Christopher Heng Chiu → Herwig Hauser: https://www.vsmath.at/?people=chiu-christopher Origin: Question arises from the author’s new comparison theorem. Joint formulation with Tommaso de Fernex and Roi Docampo appeared in their January 2020 preprint, disclosed in the thesis introduction.

**Source.** Christopher Heng Chiu. *Local geometry of the space of arcs*. University of Vienna, 2020. Advisor(s): Herwig Hauser. [primary source](https://homepage.univie.ac.at/herwig.hauser/Publications/chiu%20dissertation%202020.pdf) · [record](https://www.vsmath.at/?people=chiu-christopher) Location: Question 2.5.24, printed p. 86; Definitions 2.5.1, 2.5.12 and 1.3.32.

**Further links.** [1](https://homepage.univie.ac.at/herwig.hauser/Publications/yurkevich%20dissertation%202023.pdf) · [2](https://doi.org/10.1017/fmp.2021.19) · [3](https://www.cambridge.org/core/services/aop-cambridge-core/content/view/718B33B69515A2C0B476099F6A09B391/S2050508621000196a.pdf/embedding_codimension_of_the_space_of_arcs.pdf)

## Q1103. Poisson and quantum isomorphism groupoids

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 6.2.5) · **Collection** 12

Are PIso(V/G) and QIso(V/G) isomorphic over the common parameter space P/W?

**Context.** Origin for question 1103: Castellan’s symplectic-quotient extension of the Belov-Kanel–Kontsevich problem. Setup for question 1103: For a finite-dimensional complex symplectic V and finite G≤Sp(V), filtered Poisson deformations A_c of C[V]^G and filtered quantizations U_c share the parameter space P/W. Let PIso and QIso have these parameters as objects and all Poisson, respectively associative, algebra isomorphisms as morphisms, without filtration-preservation requirements.

**Source.** Simone Castellan. *Chiralization of star-products and the isomorphisms of deformations and quantizations of Kleinian singularities*. 2024. Advisor(s): Gwyn Bellamy; Daniele Valeri. [primary source](https://theses.gla.ac.uk/84702/1/2024CastellanPhD.pdf) Location: Conjecture 6.2.5, printed p.108, with §6.2 definitions; parameter-compatible wording reaffirmed in Bellamy 2026, Question 7.6.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1103, checked 6 October 2026: A/D Kleinian cases are proved. Bellamy’s 2026 Question 7.6 retains the general groupoid target; the solved affine-space BKK case does not settle arbitrary finite G.

**Further links.** [1](https://sites.google.com/view/simonecastellan-math/home) · [2](https://londmathsoc.onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/full/10.1112/jlms.70373) · [3](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00031-024-09879-8)

## Q1104. Divided-difference formula for two-row Springer components

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 2) · **Collection** 12

For every σ and every ordering (i₁,j₁),…,(i_ℓ,j_ℓ) of its cups that places nested cups before containing cups, is [B_σ]=(∑\_{a=i₁}^{j₁−1}∂\_a)∘···∘(∑\_{a=i_ℓ}^{j_ℓ−1}∂\_a)(S_{w₀}) in H\*(Fl_n(C),Z)?

**Context.** Origin for question 1104: Thesis-related joint conjecture; repository abstract confirms dissertation formulas, but embargoed thesis text was not inspected. Setup for question 1104: Let ℓ≥1 and n=2ℓ. For a standard tableau σ of shape (ℓ,ℓ), B_σ is the corresponding component of the complex Springer fiber of Jordan type (ℓ,ℓ). Its noncrossing matching pairs bottom-row entries as right endpoints. Write ∂\_i f=(f−s_i f)/(x_i−x_{i+1}) and S_{w₀}=x₁^{n−1}x₂^{n−2}···x_{n−1}.

**Source.** Cristina Sabando-Alvarez; Martha Precup. *Ideals defining components of two-row Springer fibers*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.07507v1) Location: Conjecture 2 / Conjecture 7.5.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1104, checked 6 October 2026: June 2026 paper proves the one-big-cup cases; general formula remains conjectural. No later resolution located.

**Further links.** [1](https://repository.library.washu.edu/art_sci_etds/3829/) · [2](https://sites.google.com/view/csabandoalvarez/research) · [3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.07507) · [4](https://math.washu.edu/phd-recipients)


<a id="q1106"></a>

## Q1106. Sylvester matroids and linear presentation

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 4.1) · **Collection** 12

If |M|≥3, I_M is quadratic, and every pair of elements belongs to a three-element circuit, must I_M be linearly presented?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1106, 1107: Conjecture 4.1 originates in this paper; Question 4.5 restates McCullough’s 2021 Question 7.10. Setup for questions 1106, 1107: For the matroid question, let M be an arbitrary simple matroid and k a characteristic-zero field. GMA(M) has basis y_F over flats, with y_Fy_G=y_{F∨G} when ranks add, and 0 otherwise; I_M is its defining ideal in rank-one-flat variables. “Linearly presented” means its first syzygies are linear. The regularity question concerns polynomial rings over a field k.

**Source.** Louiza Fouli; Sean Grate; Selvi Kara; Adam LaClair; Jason McCullough; Vinh Nguyen; Aleksandra Sobieska; Prajwal Udanshive. *Graded Betti numbers of graded Möbius algebras of uniform matroids*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/html/2609.03827v1) Location: Conjecture 4.1; Proposition 4.4 proves the converse.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1106, 1107, checked 6 October 2026: September 2026 paper retains both targets; it proves only necessity in Conjecture 4.1 and the Möbius-algebra case of Question 4.5. No resolution located.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.03827)

## Q1115. Subnormalizer character-value correspondence

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1) · **Collection** 12

For every finite G, prime p, and p-element x, is there a bijection f:Irr^x(G)→Irr^x(Sub_G(x)) with χ(1)\_p=f(χ)(1)\_p and Q(χ(x))=Q(f(χ)(x)) for every χ?

**Context.** Origin for question 1115: Moretó–Rizo conjecture, recorded by Malle; not Malle’s original conjecture. Setup for question 1115: For finite G and a p-element x, set Irr^x(G)={χ∈Irr(G):χ(x)≠0} and Sub_G(x)=⟨g∈G:⟨x⟩ is subnormal in ⟨x,g⟩⟩. Characters are complex, and a_p denotes the p-part of a positive integer a.

**Source.** Gunter Malle. *Picky elements, subnormalisers, and character correspondences*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/html/2503.10425v2) Location: Conjecture 1.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1115, checked 6 October 2026: Reaffirmed by Moretó in April/May 2026. Subsequent results cover certain p-solvable and symmetric-group cases, not the general statement.

**Further links.** [1](https://doi.org/10.1017/fms.2025.10117) · [2](https://arxiv.org/html/2604.24565v1) · [3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.11988) · [4](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.22669)


<a id="q1116"></a>

## Q1116. Isaacs–Seitz derived-length bound

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 4.1) · **Collection** 12

Does dl(G)≤|cd(G)| hold for every finite solvable group G?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1116, 1117: The Isaacs–Seitz derived-length and Eaton–Moretó degree-height conjectures are restated by Moretó. Setup for questions 1116, 1117: All groups are finite. Put cd(H)={χ(1):χ∈Irr(H)}, with complex irreducible characters, and let dl(H) be derived length. For a p-block B with defect group D, ht(B) is its set of character heights; inf ∅=∞.

**Source.** Alexander Moretó. *The Main Problem of Block Theory: Picky Elements and Subnormalizers*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/html/2604.24565v1) Location: Conjecture 4.1.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1116, 1117, checked 6 October 2026: April 2026 source retains both. The claimed p-solvable Eaton–Moretó proof was withdrawn in September for an error. August work proves one principal-block inequality; the all-blocks question remains open in the searched literature.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.24565) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.22813) · [3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.16398)

## Q1201. Zero–one dichotomy for content

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question II.0.5) · **Collection** 13

Must h_f^d(R) belong to {0,1} for every such R and f, including non-Noetherian R?

**Context.** Origin for question 1201: Hochster–Huneke’s older content question, restated in this thesis. Setup for question 1201: For a commutative unital ring R and f=(f₁,…,f_d), d≥1, set I=(f₁,…,f_d), I_k=(f₁^{k₁},…,f_d^{k_d}). Let L_I(M) be the minimum length of a filtration of M whose factors are cyclic and annihilated by I. Define h_f^d(R)=lim_{s→∞} inf_{k_i≥s} L_I(R/I_k)/(k₁⋯k_d).

**Source.** Olivia J. Strahan. *Combinatorial Methods in Mixed Characteristic: The Theory of t-Monomials*. 2025. Advisor(s): Karen E. Smith; Mel Hochster. [primary source](https://sites.lsa.umich.edu/kesmith/wp-content/uploads/sites/1309/2025/07/ostrah_thesis_with_grant_acknowledgements.pdf) Location: Question II.0.5; Definitions II.0.1–II.0.4, pp.4–5.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1201, checked 6 October 2026: The thesis and 2025 journal paper leave the general dichotomy open; searched later work gives special ring classes, not a resolution.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.19937) · [2](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jalgebra.2024.11.022) · [3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.38947)


<a id="q1202"></a>

## Q1202. ACC for skew subbraces versus underlying subgroups

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 3.2.15) · **Collection** 13

If a two-sided skew brace A satisfies ACC on skew subbraces, must both underlying groups satisfy ACC on subgroups?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1202, 1203: First: Trappeniers’s doctoral research, also in his 2023 paper. Second: older radical-ring question linked to Sysak (2012). Setup for questions 1202, 1203: A two-sided skew brace has group laws ·,∘ satisfying a∘(b·c)=(a∘b)·a⁻¹·(a∘c) and (a·b)∘c=(a∘c)·c⁻¹·(b∘c). A brace additionally has abelian (A,·). A skew subbrace is a subgroup under both laws. ACC means no infinite strictly ascending chain.

**Source.** Senne Trappeniers. *Skew braces and their connection to the Yang-Baxter equation, Hopf-Galois structures and post-Lie rings*. 2025. Advisor(s): Leandro Vendramin; Arne Van Antwerpen. [primary source](https://leandrovendramin.org/files/trappeniers.pdf) Location: Question 3.2.15, p.77.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1202, 1203, checked 6 October 2026: Both remain unanswered in the 2025 thesis; targeted searches found no later resolution.

**Further links.** [1](https://researchportal.vub.be/en/publications/skew-braces-and-their-connection-to-the-yang-baxter-equation-hopf/) · [2](https://leandrovendramin.org/students.html) · [3](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jalgebra.2023.05.003)

Can a two-sided brace have finitely generated (A,∘) but non-finitely-generated (A,·)?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1202, 1203: First: Trappeniers’s doctoral research, also in his 2023 paper. Second: older radical-ring question linked to Sysak (2012). A skew subbrace is a subgroup under both laws. ACC means no infinite strictly ascending chain.

## Q1204. Koszulness of the Filippov 3-Lie operad

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 13

Is Fil₃ Koszul?

**Context.** Origin for question 1204: Older operadic problem, explicitly restated as open; not attributed originally to Qin. Setup for question 1204: Over a characteristic-zero field, let Fil₃ be the quadratic operad generated by a skew-symmetric ternary bracket satisfying [x,y,[u,v,w]]=[[x,y,u],v,w]+[u,[x,y,v],w]+[u,v,[x,y,w]].

**Source.** Yufei Qin. *Research on algebraic structures related to solutions of (quantum) Yang-Baxter equation*. 2026. Advisor(s): Leandro Vendramin; Guodong Zhou. [primary source](https://leandrovendramin.org/files/qin.pdf) Location: Introduction §1.1.2, final paragraph; Definition 2.1.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1204, checked 6 October 2026: Explicitly open in June 2026 thesis. Tipton computes the quadratic dual but does not prove Koszulness; no later resolution located.

<a id="q1208"></a>

## Q1208. Retraction for cyclic diagonals of odd composite size

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 6.2) · **Collection** 13

For distinct odd primes p,q, must every cycle set of cardinality n=p²q whose diagonal T is an n-cycle be retractable?

**Context.** Origin for question 1208: Author’s explicitly proposed odd-cardinality direction of Problem 6.2, reached through the Vendramin research network. Setup for question 1208: A finite nondegenerate cycle set (X,\*) has bijections σ\_x(y)=x\*y and T(x)=x\*x and satisfies (x\*y)\*(x\*z)=(y\*x)\*(y\*z). It is retractable when σ\_x=σ\_y for some distinct x,y.

**Source.** Carsten Dietzel. *Endocabling of involutive solutions to the Yang-Baxter equation, with an application to solutions whose diagonal is a cyclic permutation*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.14339) Location: Problem 6.2 and following paragraph, pp.23–24.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1208, checked 6 October 2026: Odd prime powers and squarefree sizes are known. The July 2026 quasilinear retraction theorem requires additional structure and does not settle arbitrary cyclic-diagonal sets.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.08609) · [2](https://sites.google.com/view/carstendietzel/startseite)


<a id="q1210"></a>

## Q1210. Quadrics minimize Hilbert–Kunz multiplicity

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1.1) · **Collection** 13

For every odd prime p and every formally unmixed nonregular Noetherian local ring R of characteristic p and dimension n, must e_HK(R)≥e_HK(S_{p,n,2})?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1210, 1211: First: Watanabe–Yoshida (2005). Second: Meng’s conjecture, retaining its unresolved F-signature component. Setup for questions 1210, 1211: Let S_{p,n,d}=F_p[[x₀,…,x_n]]/(x₀^d+⋯+x_n^d). Write e_HK for Hilbert–Kunz multiplicity and s for F-signature. A local ring is formally unmixed when every associated prime of its completion has full dimension.

**Source.** Cheng Meng. *Limits of F-invariants and Riemann-Stieltjes integral*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/html/2507.13898v3) Location: Conjecture 1.1(1).

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1210, 1211, checked 6 October 2026: Meng settles the quadric-versus-limit inequality, not the general minimization. Castillo-Rey (September 30,2026) settles the Hilbert–Kunz generating-function component only; Remark 5.12 leaves F-signatures uncomputed.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.13898) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.04346) · [3](https://arxiv.org/html/2609.39133v1)

A local ring is formally unmixed when every associated prime of its completion has full dimension.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1210, 1211, checked 6 October 2026: Meng settles the quadric-versus-limit inequality, not the general minimization. Castillo-Rey (September 30,2026) settles the Hilbert–Kunz generating-function component only; Remark 5.12 leaves F-signatures uncomputed.

## Q1216. The dihedral permutation-group size conjecture outside its exception

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 4.33) · **Collection** 13

For n≥3 with n≠4, does G≅D_n force |X|=2n for every indecomposable solution?

**Context.** Origin for question 1216: Ramírez’s doctoral research conjecture with the known exceptional case explicitly removed. Setup for question 1216: A solution here is a finite nondegenerate involutive bijection r:X²→X² satisfying r₁₂r₂₃r₁₂=r₂₃r₁₂r₂₃. Write r(x,y)=(σ\_x(y),τ\_y(x)); all σ\_x,τ\_y are permutations. Its permutation group is G=⟨σ\_x:x∈X⟩. Indecomposable means G acts transitively. D_n denotes the dihedral group of order 2n.

**Source.** Santiago Agustín Ramírez. *Invariante diagonal y cableado de soluciones a la ecuación de Yang–Baxter*. 2025. Advisor(s): Leandro Vendramin. [primary source](https://bibliotecadigital.exactas.uba.ar/download/tesis/tesis_n7849_Ramirez.pdf) Location: Conjecture 4.33, p.72; §4.5 conventions.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1216, checked 6 October 2026: Odd n is proved. Kanrar–Rump’s counterexample has n=4, which this thesis formulation excludes. No later resolution for all remaining even n was located.

**Further links.** [1](https://bibliotecadigital.exactas.uba.ar/collection/tesis/document/tesis_n7849_Ramirez) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2312.06691) · [3](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jalgebra.2024.04.032)

## Q1303. Minimum generating sets mostly inside a prescribed maximal subgroup

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 14

For every maximal subgroup M&lt;G, does G have a d(G)-element generating set with exactly d(G)−1 elements in M?

**Context.** Origin for question 1303: Authors’ conjecture following Theorem 1.3 and Corollary 1.4. Setup for question 1303: For a finite group G, d(G) is its minimum number of generators.

## Q1304. Divisibility of crossed-homomorphism counts

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 14

Is the number of crossed homomorphisms M→H always divisible by gcd(|M|,|H|)?

**Context.** Origin for question 1304: Asai–Yoshida 1993 conjecture, restated here. Setup for question 1304: Let a finite abelian group M act on a finite group H on the right. A crossed homomorphism α:M→H satisfies α(ab)=α(a)^b α(b).

**Source.** Alexander V. Khudyakov. *Another article on the number of homomorphisms*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/html/2511.16552v3) Location: §1, Asai–Yoshida conjecture; Definition 2.1.

<a id="q1305"></a>

## Q1305. Equivalence of associative and Jordan zero-product determination

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 2.7) · **Collection** 14

Is A zpd if and only if A is zJpd?

**Context.** Origin for question 1305: Conjecture 2.7 of this paper, beyond the finite-dimensional equivalence proved there. Setup for question 1305: Let A be an associative unital algebra over a field F with char F≠2, possibly infinite-dimensional. For a bilinear product μ, call A zero-product determined if every F-bilinear b:A×A→F vanishing whenever μ(x, y)=0 equals φ(μ(x, y)) for some linear φ:A→F. Use μ(x, y)=xy for zpd and μ(x, y)=xy+yx for zJpd.

**Source.** Hongyu Jia; Zhankui Xiao. *Finite dimensional zero Jordan product determined algebras are generated by idempotents*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/html/2607.05941v1) Location: Conjecture 2.7; equations(1.1)–(1.2).

**Literature check.** Status for question 1305, checked 6 October 2026: July 2026 paper proves only the finite-dimensional case; no general equivalence or counterexample located.


<a id="q1306"></a>

## Q1306. Finite-dimensionality for finitely generated Jordan-type algebras

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 4.9) · **Collection** 14

Is every n-axis-generated algebra of Jordan type finite-dimensional, with dimension bounded by a function C(n)?

**Context.** Origin for question 1306: Problem 4.9 is credited to Y. Segev. Setup for question 1306: A primitive axial F-algebra is commutative, generated by idempotents a with diagonalizable multiplication and eigenspaces A_λ(a), A₁(a)=Fa, obeying a common fusion law λ\*μ⊆F: A_λA_μ⊆⊕\_{ν∈λ\*μ}A_ν. Jordan type η≠0, 1 uses {1, 0,η} with 1\*1={1}, 1\*0=∅, 0\*0={0}, 1\*η=0\*η={η}, η\*η={1, 0}.

**Source.** Ilya Gorshkov; Sergey Shpectorov. *Axial Algebras: Questions and Conjectures*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.30048v1) Location: Problem 4.9; Definitions 2.3–2.5.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1306, checked 6 October 2026: June 2026 source explicitly retains the question; no later resolution found.

## Q1307. Local Tor-length lower bounds

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 5.1) · **Collection** 14

For every 0≤i≤k, must ℓ\_R(Tor_i^R(R/I, R/J))≥binom(k, i)ℓ\_R(R/(I+J))?

**Context.** Origin for question 1307: New lower-bound formulation after counterexamples to an older equality. Setup for question 1307: Let (R, m) be a regular local ring of dimension n. Let proper ideals I, J contain R-regular sequences of lengths k≤n and n, respectively, with I+J m-primary. Let ℓ\_R denote module length.

**Source.** Alberto F. Boix; Bárbara K. Lima–Pereira. *Exploring a local variant of the Buchsbaum–Eisenbud–Horrocks conjecture*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/html/2607.26118v1) Location: Question 5.1.

## Q1308. Symbolic regularity slope of the net graph

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 6.1) · **Collection** 14

Does lim_{m→∞} reg(J_N^{(m)})/m=3?

**Context.** Origin for question 1308: Conjecture 6.1 proposed by the authors. Setup for question 1308: Let k be a field and N the six-vertex net graph: a triangle with one pendant edge at each triangle vertex. In S=k[x₁,…, x₆, y₁,…, y₆], put J_N=(x_i y_j−x_j y_i:{i, j}∈E(N)); J_N^{(m)} denotes its m-th symbolic power.

**Source.** Dennis Belotserkovskiy; Mariana Landín; Charlie Ruppe; Lizzy Teryoshin. *Asymptotic invariants of symbolic powers of binomial edge ideals*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/html/2510.14272v1) Location: Conjecture 6.1; Definition 2.13 and Figure2.

## Q1313. Geometric realization of small Jacobi algebras

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1.11) · **Collection** 14

If Jdim(J_f)≤1, is J_f a contraction algebra of a smooth crepant projective birational morphism X→Spec R, with R a complete-local cDV threefold singularity and reduced closed fiber P¹?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1313, 1314: Brown–Wemyss realization; older noncommutative-deformation contractibility question. Found via Hao Zhang’s 2025 Glasgow thesis (Wemyss primary, Brown secondary). Setup for questions 1313, 1314: For f∈C⟨⟨x₁,…, x_d⟩⟩\_{≥2}, set J_f=C⟨⟨x⟩⟩/closure(∂\_{x₁}f,…,∂\_{x_d}f), using cyclic derivatives. For its Jacobson radical r, Jdim(J_f)=inf{s: dim_C(J_f/r^n)≤Cn^s for some C and all n≥1}.

**Source.** Gavin Brown; Michael Wemyss. *Local Normal Forms of Noncommutative Functions*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/html/2111.05900v4) Location: Conjecture 1.11; §1.5; Corollary 8.18.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1313, 1314, checked 6 October 2026: Zhang’s 2026 result covers Type A realization only; general realization and the(−3, 1) contractibility case remain beyond verified results.

## Q1314. Contractibility detected by noncommutative deformation

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 5.7) · **Collection** 14

For C≅P¹ in a smooth complex Calabi–Yau threefold X with normal bundle O(−3)⊕O(1), let Λ\_def be the noncommutative deformation algebra of O_C(−1). Does dim_C Λ\_def<∞ imply that C contracts to a point in a suitable local neighborhood, with an isomorphism elsewhere?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1313, 1314: Brown–Wemyss realization; older noncommutative-deformation contractibility question. Found via Hao Zhang’s 2025 Glasgow thesis (Wemyss primary, Brown secondary). Setup for questions 1313, 1314: For f∈C⟨⟨x₁,…, x_d⟩⟩\_{≥2}, set J_f=C⟨⟨x⟩⟩/closure(∂\_{x₁}f,…,∂\_{x_d}f), using cyclic derivatives. For its Jacobson radical r, Jdim(J_f)=inf{s: dim_C(J_f/r^n)≤Cn^s for some C and all n≥1}.

**Source.** Gavin Brown; Michael Wemyss. *Local Normal Forms of Noncommutative Functions*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/html/2111.05900v4) Location: §1.7; Theorem 1.10 and Appendix A.6; companion survey Conjecture 5.7.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1313, 1314, checked 6 October 2026: Zhang’s 2026 result covers Type A realization only; general realization and the(−3, 1) contractibility case remain beyond verified results.

**Further links.** [1](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00209-026-04003-w) · [2](https://www.maths.gla.ac.uk/~mwemyss/ECM_281024.pdf)

## Q1316. Finitistic dimension of Artin algebras

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 14

For every Artin algebra A, is sup{pd_A M:M is a finitely generated right A-module and pd_A M<∞}<∞?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1316, 1317: Classical finitistic-dimension and Gorenstein-symmetry conjectures, restated separately from the equivalences proved here. Setup for questions 1316, 1317: An Artin R-algebra A is associative and unital, finite as a module over a commutative Artinian ring R. Modules below are finitely generated.

**Source.** Haruhisa Enomoto; René Marczinzik. *On the homological conjectures for Artin algebras*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/html/2609.19172v2) Location: Introduction, FDC; equation defining findim A.

<a id="q1404"></a>

## Q1404. Extremal spacewise cross-intersection in general linear groups

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 5.5.2) · **Collection** 15

Fix an integer t≥1. For all sufficiently large n relative to t, suppose Y,Z⊂GL_n(F_q) satisfy: for every x∈Y,y∈Z some t-dimensional U≤F_q^n has xU=yU. If |Y||Z|=B(n,t,q)², must Y=Z be a coset of a setwise stabilizer of a t-space or an (n−t)-space?

**Context.** Origin for question 1404: Ernst–Schmidt’s 2023 intersection conjecture, restated in the dissertation. Setup for question 1404: For a prime power q, let B(n,t,q)=∏\_{i=0}^{t−1}(q^t−q^i)∏\_{i=t}^{n−1}(q^n−q^i).

**Source.** Alena Ernst. *Subsets of finite general linear groups*. 2024. Advisor(s): Kai-Uwe Schmidt and Margit Rösler. [primary source](https://d-nb.info/1358632553/34) Location: Conjecture 5.5.2, p. 116; Definitions 5.1.7/10.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1404, checked 6 October 2026: Pointwise-intersection results do not settle this spacewise target. No matching resolution or erratum found.

**Further links.** [1](https://digital.ub.uni-paderborn.de/hs/content/titleinfo/7844090) · [2](https://doi.org/10.1017/S0305004123000075)


<a id="q1405"></a>

## Q1405. Constant-factor cost of conjugacy in dominating sets

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 2.18) · **Collection** 15

Does an absolute c>0 satisfy γ\_u(G)≤c·γ\_t(G) for every finite nonabelian simple group G?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1405, 1406, 1407, 1408: Explicit open questions in Harper’s survey. Setup for questions 1405, 1406, 1407, 1408: s(G) is the largest k such that every x₁,…,x_k∈G\\{1} admit y with ⟨x_i,y⟩=G for all i. For u(G), y must belong to one conjugacy class fixed independently of the tuple. Define γ\_t(G)=min{|S|:∀x≠1,∃y∈S,⟨x,y⟩=G}; γ\_u(G) additionally requires all members of S to be conjugate.

**Source.** Scott Harper. *The spread of finite and infinite groups*. 2022. [primary source](https://research-repository.st-andrews.ac.uk/bitstream/handle/10023/32189/Harper-2025-The-spread-of-finite-Ch3-AAM.pdf?sequence=1) Location: Question 2.18.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1405, 1406, 1407, 1408, checked 6 October 2026: Current accepted manuscript and author bibliography checked; no matching general resolution found.

**Results.**

- **Investigated, unresolved** (B13: unresolved): No new exact-scope result established in this return. Remaining/limits: The entire retained question remains unresolved in this return. [Report](../../solutions/b13-2026-10-09/README.md).

**Further links.** [1](https://doi.org/10.1017/9781009563208.004) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2210.09635) · [3](https://harper-scott.github.io/research.html)

## Q1409. Higman’s PORC conjecture

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 15

For each fixed integer m≥1, do there exist an integer N≥1 and rational polynomials P₀,…,P_{N−1} such that g(p^m)=P_r(p) for every prime p≡r mod N?

## Q1411. Connectivity after deleting nongenerating vertices

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 2.37) · **Collection** 15

Is Δ(G) connected for every finite group G with minimum generating-set size d(G)=2?

**Context.** Origin for question 1411: Crestani–Lucchini’s earlier connectivity conjecture, restated and partially advanced. Setup for question 1411: The generating graph has vertices G and edges between distinct x,y with ⟨x,y⟩=G; Δ(G) is its nonisolated induced subgraph.

**Source.** Andrea Lucchini; Daniele Nemmi. *On the connectivity of the generating and rank graphs of finite groups*. 2025. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.16427) Location: Introduction, connectivity conjecture; compare Harper Conjecture 2.37.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1411, checked 6 October 2026: The paper settles almost all almost-simple cases and reduces to groups with trivial soluble radical; the general conjecture remains.

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 3) · **Collection** 15

## Q1415. Cyclicity of torsion Weyl-algebra modules

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 3.4) · **Collection** 15

For every n≥1, is every finitely generated torsion left A_n-module cyclic?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1415, 1416: Stafford’s 1978 conjecture and Bellamy’s Ext question, respectively. Setup for questions 1415, 1416: A_n is the complex n-th Weyl algebra. For the Ext question, U is a filtered C-algebra with gr U=C[X] as graded Poisson algebras, for a conic symplectic singularity X. Ch(M)=Supp(gr M); holonomic means its intersection with each symplectic leaf is Lagrangian or empty.

**Source.** Gwyn Bellamy. *Module structure of Weyl algebras*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/html/2510.19344v1) Location: Conjecture 3.4.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1415, 1416, checked 6 October 2026: The 2026 publication retains both targets. Bellamy–Etingof–Thompson prove finite Ext for Cherednik algebras under extra hypotheses, not arbitrary conic symplectic quantizations; no full resolution located.

**Further links.** [1](https://eprints.gla.ac.uk/372520/) · [2](https://doi.org/10.1112/jlms.70373) · [3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.18210)

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 2) · **Collection** 16

For every uniform pro-p group G and nonempty finite A,B⊂G, must some element of AB have exactly one representation ab with a∈A,b∈B?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1506, 1507: Unique products: Craig–Linnell (2020); free profinite indicability: Jaikin-Zapirain (2023). Setup for questions 1502, 1506, 1507: A uniform pro-p group is torsion-free, topologically finitely generated and powerful: [G,G]⊆G^p (G⁴ for p=2), using closed power subgroups. Local indicability concerns abstract groups.

**Source.** Martina Conte. *On some algebraic properties of p-adic analytic pro-p groups*. 2023. Advisor(s): Benjamin Klopsch; second advisor Holger Kammeyer. [primary source](https://docserv.uni-duesseldorf.de/servlets/DerivateServlet/Derivate-71068/PhD%20Thesis%20Conte%20Martina.pdf) Location: Chapter 3, Conjecture 2, p. 82; §3.6, p. 113.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1506, 1507, checked 6 October 2026: The thesis proves soluble cases; targeted searches found no general resolution.

**Further links.** [1](https://docserv.uni-duesseldorf.de/servlets/DocumentServlet?id=65729) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2003.04945) · [3](https://doi.org/10.1007/s40687-023-00408-9)

<a id="q1515"></a>

## Q1515. Kaplansky zero-divisors

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 16

For every field K and torsion-free group G, does ab=0 in K[G] force a=0 or b=0?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1515, 1516, 1517: Classical Kaplansky problems; zero-divisors trace back to Higman’s 1940 thesis. Direct and stable finiteness are equivalent globally and counted once. Setup for questions 1515, 1516, 1517: K[G] denotes the ordinary finite-support group algebra, with convolution multiplication. Matrix identities use I_d.

**Source.** Tullio Ceccherini-Silberstein, Michel Coornaert, Xuan Kien Phung. *First-order model theory and Kaplansky’s stable finiteness conjecture for surjunctive groups*. 2025. [primary source](https://ems.press/content/serial-article-files/51166?nt=1) Location: Remark 4.4, p. 501.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1515, 1516, 1517, checked 6 October 2026: 2025–2026 sources reaffirm these problems; special group classes are settled. Gardam disproved only the separate unit conjecture.

**Further links.** [1](https://doi.org/10.4171/GGD/885) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.07646) · [3](https://ems.press/content/serial-article-files/52395?nt=1) · [4](https://www.aimspress.com/article/doi/10.3934/math.2026014) · [5](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00208-026-03403-x)

## Q1601. Nilpotent post-Lie left multiplications

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 6.12) · **Collection** 17

If g and n are nilpotent stem Lie algebras, must every left multiplication L(x):y↦x·y be nilpotent?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1601, 1602: Origins: Burde–Ender–Moens (2018); Burde–Dekimpe–Deschamps (2004/2005). Setup for question 1601: For finite-dimensional complex Lie algebras g=(V,[,]) and n=(V,{,}), a post-Lie product satisfies x·y−y·x=[x,y]−{x,y}, [x,y]·z=x·(y·z)−y·(x·z), and x·{y,z}={x·y,z}+{y,x·z}. Stem means Z(g)⊆[g,g].

**Source.** Christof Ender. *Post-Lie algebra structures on classes of Lie algebras*. 2019. Advisor(s): Dietrich Burde. [primary source](https://homepage.univie.ac.at/Dietrich.Burde/papers/thesis_ender_2019.pdf) Location: Question 6.12, p. 146.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1601, 1602, checked 6 October 2026: Known nilpotent and low-dimensional results are partial; targeted searches found no general resolution.

## Q1603. Singleton biclosed covers

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 3.0.5) · **Collection** 17

Does every cover B₁⋖B₂ in Bic(W) satisfy |B₂∖B₁|=1?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1603, 1604, 1605, 1606: Earlier conjectures of Dyer, Barkley–Speyer, and Lusztig–Dyer, respectively. Setup for questions 1603, 1604, 1605: Let Φ⁺ be positive roots for a reduced based real root system of W. B⊆Φ⁺ is biclosed when B and its complement contain every positive root in each two-element nonnegative span. Bic(W) orders these sets by inclusion.

**Source.** Grant T. Barkley. *Infinite root systems in algebra and geometry*. 2025. Advisor(s): Lauren K. Williams. [primary source](https://gtbarkley.org/papers/Thesis.pdf) Location: §3.0, p. 47, Conjecture 3.0.5 and equivalence.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1603, 1604, 1605, 1606, checked 6 October 2026: 2026 results cover U₃ and bounded Bruhat-interval lengths; the general assertions remain open.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/html/2509.00871v2) · [2](https://arxiv.org/html/2410.11717v1) · [3](https://arxiv.org/html/2601.07793v1) · [4](https://arxiv.org/html/2509.16433v2)

## Q1614. Central free-nilpotent CPA structures

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 4.9) · **Collection** 17

For every r≥2 and c≥3, must every CPA product on F_{r,c} satisfy F_{r,c}·F_{r,c}⊆Z(F_{r,c})?

**Context.** Origin for question 1614: Authors’ conjectures; encountered through Ender’s dissertation. Setup for question 1614: Over a characteristic-zero field, all Lie algebras are finite-dimensional. A CPA product is commutative and satisfies [x,y]·z=x·(y·z)−y·(x·z) and x·[y,z]=[x·y,z]+[y,x·z]. F_{r,c} is free nilpotent on r generators, of class c.

**Source.** Dietrich Burde, Karel Dekimpe, Wolfgang Alexander Moens. *Commutative post-Lie algebra structures and linear equations for nilpotent Lie algebras*. 2019. [primary source](https://homepage.univie.ac.at/dietrich.burde/papers/burde_57_cpa.pdf) Location: Conjecture 4.9, p. 11.

## Q1615. Minimal resolutions of weighted Veronese embeddings

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 3) · **Collection** 17

Determine the minimal graded free resolution of I_d for arbitrary n, weights and such d.

**Context.** Origin for question 1615: Author’s explicit open problem, encountered through his doctoral work; paper first appeared in 2024, before the 2025 thesis. Setup for question 1615: Over an algebraically closed field k of characteristic zero, let S=k[x₀,…, x_n], deg x₀=1, deg x_i=a_i>0, and let d>0 be a sufficiently large multiple of lcm(a₁,…,a_n) for the Veronese map to be an embedding. Write m₀,…, m_N for all degree-d monomials of S and I_d=ker(k[y₀,…, y_N]→S), y_i↦m_i.

**Source.** Shahriyar Roshan Zamir. *Interpolation in Weighted Projective Spaces*. 2025. Advisor(s): Alexandra Seceleanu. [primary source](https://digitalcommons.unl.edu/cgi/viewcontent.cgi?article=1386&context=dissunl) Location: Chapter 9, Problem 3, p. 87; Definition 8.4, p. 72.

<a id="q1616"></a>

## Q1616. Excellent generically Gorenstein failure of total acyclicity

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 17

Does there exist an excellent generically Gorenstein ring R that fails (tac)?

**Context.** Origin for question 1616: Explicit unresolved excellent-case question in Remark 2.5, after a prior unrestricted claim was refuted. Setup for question 1616: Let R be a commutative Noetherian ring, either local or positively graded over a field. It satisfies (tac) when Hom_R(P,R) is acyclic for every acyclic complex P of finitely generated projective R-modules, using graded complexes in the graded case. Generically Gorenstein means R_p is Gorenstein for every associated prime p∈Ass(R).

**Source.** Justin Lyle. *On The Totally Acyclic Condition and the Auslander-Reiten Conjecture*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/html/2610.00022v1) Location: Remark 2.5 and Definition 2.2.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1616, checked 6 October 2026: The 2026 paper distinguishes known non-excellent counterexamples from the still-unknown excellent case; no later resolution found.

**Further links.** [1](https://www.math.nagoya-u.ac.jp/~takahashi/ufd20.pdf)


<a id="q1660"></a>

## Q1660. Construct Ramsey Cayley graphs on every group

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 1.9) · **Collection** 17

Is there an absolute C such that every finite group G of order N≥2 admits an inverse-closed S⊆G∖{1} for which both the clique number and independence number of Cay(G,S) are at most C log₂N?

**Context.** Origin for question 1660: Cayley-graph conjecture due to Noga Alon; subspace conjecture newly proposed as Conjecture 5.1 by Conlon–Fox–Pham–Yepremyan. Setup for question 1660: For a finite group G and inverse-closed S⊆G∖{1}, Cay(G,S) joins distinct x,y when xy⁻¹∈S.

**Source.** David Conlon; Jacob Fox; Huy Tuan Pham; Liana Yepremyan. *On the clique number of random Cayley graphs and related topics*. 2024. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.21194) Location: Conjecture 1.9.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1660, checked 6 October 2026: The primary paper retains both questions; January 2026 Stanford seminar by Carl Schildkraut reports extensions to many nonabelian groups, rather than a complete solution. Searches through 6 October 2026 found no general resolution. The paper specifically leaves characteristic-two and characteristic-three vector groups open.

**Further links.** [1](https://www.its.caltech.edu/~dconlon/random-cayley.pdf) · [2](https://mathematics.stanford.edu/events/ramsey-cayley-graphs-over-non-abelian-groups)

## Q1701. Nondistributive long hooks

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 11.5) · **Collection** 18

For λ=(n−r,1^r) with 10≤r≤n−r, must S^λ have a nondistributive submodule lattice?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1701, 1702: First posed in Kapadia’s 2024 paper, Conjectures 8.2–8.3. Setup for questions 1701, 1702: Let k be a characteristic-two field and S^λ the Specht kS_n-module. A module is distributive if its submodule lattice is distributive. Set L(r)=min{a≥0:r<2^a}.

**Source.** Zain Ahmed Kapadia. *On the Submodule Structure of Hook Specht Modules in Characteristic 2*. 2025. Advisor(s): Matthew Fayers. [primary source](https://qmro.qmul.ac.uk/xmlui/bitstream/handle/123456789/108716/Zain%20Ahmed%20Kapadia%20-%20Thesis%20-%20On%20the%20Submodule%20Structure%20of%20%20Hook%20Specht%20Modules%20in%20Characteristic%202.pdf?sequence=2) Location: Thesis Conjecture 11.5, p. 125; paper Conjecture 8.2, p. 43.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1701, 1702, checked 6 October 2026: The sequel classifies sums of uniserials; these lattice conjectures remain unresolved in the checked literature.

## Q1708. Maximum relation count

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 2) · **Collection** 18

Determine sup{ρ(Γ):edim(Γ)=e, mult(Γ)=m} for all 4≤e≤m.

**Context.** Origin for questions 1708, 1709, 1710, 1711: Origins: Elmacioglu et al. (2024); Ciolan–García-Sánchez–Moree (2016); Rossi (2011). Setup for questions 1708, 1709, 1710, 1711: Over a field k, let Γ=⟨g₁<⋯&lt;g_e⟩⊂ℕ be cofinite, minimally generated; m=g₁. Put PF(Γ)={a∈ℤ∖Γ:a+(Γ∖{0})⊆Γ}, t(Γ)=|PF(Γ)|. Let ρ(Γ) count minimal binomial generators of ker(k[x₁,…,x_e]→k[t^Γ]), x_i↦t^{g_i}. Set R=k[[t^Γ]], with maximal ideal 𝔪; complete intersection means ρ=e−1.

**Source.** Alessio Moscariello; Alessio Sammartano. *Open problems on relations of numerical semigroups*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/html/2406.00790v3) Location: Problem 2; Theorems 2–6.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1708, 1709, 1710, 1711, checked 6 October 2026: September revision retains these targets; subsequent searches found no general solution.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.00790) · [2](https://arxiv.org/html/2609.04000v1) · [3](https://arxiv.org/abs/1409.5614) · [4](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.29484)

## Q1712. Five-generator type bound

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 4.2) · **Collection** 18

If S is nearly Gorenstein, is t≤5, with equality only when S is almost symmetric?

**Context.** Origin for question 1712: Authors’ Question 4.2. Setup for question 1712: Let S⊂ℕ be a cofinite additive monoid with minimal generators g₁,…,g₅. Define PF={a∈ℤ∖S:a+(S∖{0})⊆S}, F=max PF, t=|PF|. Nearly Gorenstein means every g_i admits f_i∈PF with g_i+f_i−f∈S for all f∈PF; almost symmetric means g_i+F−f∈S for all i,f∈PF.

**Source.** Alessio Moscariello; Francesco Strazzanti. *Nearly Gorenstein vs Almost Gorenstein Affine Monomial Curves*. 2021. [primary source](https://d-nb.info/1244016322/34) Location: Question 4.2, p. 11; Proposition 1.1.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1712, checked 6 October 2026: The 2025 bound is 40 in the non-almost-symmetric case; the conjectural bound five remains open.

**Further links.** [1](https://doi.org/10.1007/s00009-021-01761-1) · [2](https://arxiv.org/html/2501.08260v1) · [3](https://arxiv.org/html/2601.19629v2) · [4](https://arxiv.org/html/2406.00790v3)


<a id="q1713"></a>

## Q1713. Solvable subgroup depth bound

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** conjecture (Conjecture 20) · **Collection** 18

If G has trivial solvable radical, must d_G(H)≤9 for every solvable H≤G?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1713, 1714: Explicit questions in the paper; depth defined by Boltje–Danz–Külshammer. Setup for questions 1713, 1714: For H≤G finite, put A=ℂG, B=ℂH and T_n=A⊗\_B⋯⊗\_BA (n factors), T₀=B. The subgroup depth d_G(H) is the least admissible positive integer: 2n is admissible if T_{n+1} is a summand of finitely many copies of T_n as (A,B)-bimodule; 2n+1 uses (B,B)-bimodules.

**Source.** Timothy C. Burness. *On the depth of subgroups of simple groups*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/html/2503.23845v2) Location: Conjecture 20 in arXiv v2; journal Conjecture 1.10.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1713, 1714, checked 6 October 2026: The 2026 publication retains these cases; targeted later searches found no resolution.

**Further links.** [1](https://doi.org/10.1017/fms.2026.10174) · [2](https://seis.bristol.ac.uk/~tb13602/docs/sylow_revised.pdf) · [3](https://anton-baykalov.github.io/pdftexts/BaseUSpBaykalov.pdf) · [4](https://anton-baykalov.github.io/)

<a id="q1715"></a>

## Q1715. Characterize property-F Lie algebras

**Status:** Solved here: disproved · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 18

For nilpotent g with dim(g/[g,g])=2, suppose every generating pair x,y and every u,v,w∈[g,g] satisfying [x,u]+[y,v]=[x,v]+[y,w]=0 has u,v,w∈Z(g). Is this equivalent to g≅F_{2,c} for some c≥1?

**Context.** Origin for question 1715: Authors’ closing open problem. Setup for question 1715: Work over a characteristic-zero field with finite-dimensional Lie algebras. F_{2,c} is the free nilpotent Lie algebra on two generators of class c.

**Source.** Dietrich Burde, Karel Dekimpe, Wolfgang Alexander Moens. *Commutative post-Lie algebra structures and linear equations for nilpotent Lie algebras*. 2019. [primary source](https://homepage.univie.ac.at/dietrich.burde/papers/burde_57_cpa.pdf) Location: §5, final Open problem, p. 13.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1715, checked 6 October 2026: Known in complex dimensions at most seven; no general characterization found.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/1711.01964) · [2](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.00614)


**Result (2026-10-09).** An explicit 13-dimensional two-generated nilpotent Lie algebra of class five has property F for every generating pair over every characteristic-zero field, but is not free nilpotent. Its graded dimensions are 2,1,2,3,5 and its center has dimension five. Disproves the proposed equivalence. The universal generating-pair assertion follows from a symmetric-matrix change-of-generators argument and graded injectivity, not from a finite search. No smallest-dimension or full classification claim is made.

[Proof](../../solutions/seven-more-2026-10-09/algebra/Q1715.md) · [Internal review](../../solutions/seven-more-2026-10-09/discrete/review-Q1715.md) · [Exact computational controls](../../solutions/seven-more-2026-10-09/algebra/q1715-certificate.json) · [Source check](../../solutions/seven-more-2026-10-09/algebra/sources.md). This is a research result with a separate internal AI review, not an externally peer-reviewed or formally certified theorem.


<a id="q1716"></a>

## Q1716. Existence of characteristic-zero F-invariant limits

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 18

Do both lim_{p→∞}e_p and lim_{p→∞}s_p exist for every such R?

**Context.** Origin for question 1716: Older limit-existence problem, explicitly stated open here; not claimed as a new conjecture. Setup for question 1716: Let K have characteristic zero and R=(K[x₁,…,x_n]/I)\_(x₁,…,x_n). Choose free descent data A→R_A as in Definition 2.7. Put e_p=inf_{m∈MaxSpec(A/pA)}e_HK(R_{k(m)}) and s_p=sup_{m∈MaxSpec(A/pA)}s(R_{k(m)}), using the characteristic-p local models of §2.2. Here e_HK and s denote Hilbert–Kunz multiplicity and F-signature.

**Source.** Joel Castillo-Rey. *Limit F-invariants of the simple singularities and the Fermat hypersurfaces*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/html/2609.39133v1) Location: Introduction, paragraph 2; Definitions 2.7–2.11.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1716, checked 6 October 2026: The September 30, 2026 source explicitly says general existence remains open even for hypersurfaces; its computations cover special singularities.

**Further links.** [1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.39133)


<a id="q1717"></a>

## Q1717. A Bergman gap for axial algebras

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Problem 3.24) · **Collection** 18

Does a finitely generated primitive axial algebra exist with 1&lt;GKdim(A)<2, where GKdim(A)=limsup_{n→∞} log(dim V^{≤n})/log n for a finite-dimensional generating subspace V?

**Context.** Origin for question 1717: Problem posed by Ivan Kaygorodov. Setup for question 1717: A primitive axial F-algebra is commutative, generated by idempotents a whose multiplication operators diagonalize, with A₁(a)=Fa; a common fusion law λ\*μ prescribes A_λ(a)A_μ(a)⊆⊕\_{ν∈λ\*μ}A_ν(a).

**Source.** Ilya Gorshkov; Sergey Shpectorov. *Axial Algebras: Questions and Conjectures*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/html/2606.30048v1) Location: Problem 3.24(a); Definition 3.23.

<a id="q1762"></a>

## Q1762. Connect second neighbourhoods of covers

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 18

If r>2 and c₂>1, must X[Γ₂(v)] be connected for every vertex v, without assuming X triangle-free?

**Context.** Origin for question 1762: Levit’s future-work questions. Setup for question 1762: A drackn X is a finite connected distance-regular graph of diameter 3 that is an antipodal cover of K_n: its n fibres each have r vertices, each fibre is independent, distinct fibres induce perfect matchings, and distinct vertices in one fibre are distance 3. Distance-regular means that, for d(u,v)=i, the number of neighbours w of v with d(u,w)=j depends only on i,j. Write Γ₂(v)={x:d(v,x)=2}; c₂ is the common-neighbour count for vertices at distance 2.

**Source.** Maxwell Levit. *Graph Coverings with Few Eigenvalues or No Short Cycles*. 2023. Advisor(s): Chris Godsil. [primary source](https://dspacemainprd01.lib.uwaterloo.ca/server/api/core/bitstreams/45db744e-f09f-4d0f-906a-95c9e4d00903/content) Location: §6.6, item 2, p. 72.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1762, checked 6 October 2026: Checked 6 October 2026; no later resolution found.

**Further links.** [1](https://uwspace.uwaterloo.ca/items/76a24367-2d29-404f-973c-2e89ff68b446) · [2](https://umjuran.ru/index.php/umj/article/view/431/0) · [3](https://uwaterloo.ca/daily-bulletin/2023-04-28) · [4](https://www.math.uwaterloo.ca/~cgodsil/mine/students.html)


<a id="q1801"></a>

## Q1801. AS-Gorenstein noetherian Hopf algebras

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem · **Collection** 19

Must every such H be AS-Gorenstein?

**Context.** Origin for question 1801: Brown–Goodearl (1997), restated in the thesis. Setup for question 1801: Let H be a left and right noetherian Hopf algebra over a field k; k is the trivial H-module via the counit. AS-Gorenstein means finite self-injective dimension d on both sides, with Ext_H^i(k,H) one-dimensional over k for i=d and zero otherwise, and likewise for right modules.

**Source.** Steven W. N. O’Hagan. *Noetherian Hopf algebras and their extensions*. 2012. Advisor(s): Kenneth A. Brown. [primary source](https://theses.gla.ac.uk/3782/1/2012OHaganPhD.pdf) Location: §5.6, pp. 104–105, Definition 5.18 and paragraph before Theorem 5.22.

**Literature check.** Status for question 1801, checked 6 October 2026: Brown’s 2026 report retains the general question.

**Further links.** [1](https://theses.gla.ac.uk/3782/) · [2](https://arxiv.org/html/2605.00774v1) · [3](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.20249)


<a id="q1802"></a>

## Q1802. Hopf structure of coinvariants

**Status:** Open · **Kind:** open problem (Question 3.16) · **Collection** 19

Must C be a normal Hopf subalgebra of H?

**Context.** Origin for questions 1802, 1803, 1804, 1805: Coinvariant questions follow Lu–Wu–Zhang (2007); the growth and generation questions are listed by Brown. Setup for questions 1802, 1803, 1804, 1805: Let k be algebraically closed of characteristic zero. For the coinvariant questions, H is an affine noetherian Hopf k-algebra with finite global dimension and GKdim(H)=1. Let P₀ be its unique minimal prime contained in ker ε, π:H→H/P₀, and C={h∈H:(id⊗π)Δ(h)=h⊗1}.

**Source.** Ken A. Brown. *Report on AS-Gorenstein Hopf algebras*. 2026. [primary source](https://arxiv.org/html/2605.00774v1) Location: Question 3.16(i); Theorem 3.14.

**Literature check.** Status for questions 1802, 1803, 1804, 1805, checked 6 October 2026: The 2026 report explicitly retains these questions.
